## 1 Intr<sub>o</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n

As AI a<sub>g</sub>ents based on lar<sub>g</sub>e lan<sub>g</sub>ua<sub>g</sub>e models (LLMs) be-<sub>come</sub> <sub>ever</sub> <sub>more</sub> <sub>au</sub>t<sub>onomous</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>capa</sub>bl<sub>e,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>may</sub> <sub>even</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> see the emergence of an agentic economy, where AI agents <sub>nego</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e, coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e, an</sub>d <sub>carry ou</sub>t <sub>econom</sub>i<sub>c</sub> t<sub>ransac</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> on behalf of humans (Rothschild et al. 2025; Tomasev et al. 2025; Shahidi et al. 2025). In contrast to earlier <sub>g</sub>enerations of economic a<sub>g</sub>ents, such as al<sub>g</sub>orithmic traders (Alm<sub>g</sub>ren and Chriss 2000; Hendershott, Jones, and Menkveld 2011), auction a<sub>g</sub>ents (Stone et al. 2002), smart contracts (Szabo 1997; Werbach and Cornell 2017), and ne<sub>g</sub>otiation al<sub>g</sub>orithms (Kraus, Wilkenfeld, and Zlotkin 1995; Faratin, Sierra, <sub>a</sub>nd J<sub>e</sub>nnin<sub>gs</sub> 1998<sub>;</sub> Kr<sub>aus,</sub> S<sub>yca</sub>r<sub>a, a</sub>nd E<sub>ve</sub>n<sub>c</sub>hik 1998<sub>;</sub> J<sub>e</sub>n<sub>-</sub> nin<sub>g</sub>s et al. 2001), modern AI a<sub>g</sub>ents are distin<sub>g</sub>uished b<sub>y</sub> their ability to use natural language. This opens up the possibility <sub>o</sub>f <sub>agreemen</sub>t<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>s over a muc</sub>h <sub>w</sub>id<sub>er space o</sub>f terms, going beyond just prices and quantities to include dynamic obli<sub>g</sub>ations (Batta<sub>g</sub>lini and Lamba 2019), contin<sub>g</sub>encies (Bazerman and Gilles<sub>p</sub>ie 1999), and qualitative assurances (Bernheim and Whinston 1998; Choi and Triantis 2009). Rubinstein 1982; M<sub>y</sub>erson and Satterthwaite 1983), alon<sub>g</sub> with economic and le<sub>g</sub>al contract desi<sub>g</sub>n (Telser 1980; Hart 1988; Choi and Triantis 2009; Thomas 2022).

Strategic rationality in LLMs has been evaluated in Diplomacy, repeated games, social dilemmas, and business simulation (Bakhtin et al. 2022; Akata et al. 2023; Piatti et al. 2024; Vezhnevets et al. 2023; Backlund and Petersson 2025), and <sub>a grow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> li<sub>ne o</sub>f <sub>wor</sub>k b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s</sub> LLM <sub>nego</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> it<sub>se</sub>lf<sub>,</sub> s<sub>p</sub>annin<sub>g</sub> scorable bar<sub>g</sub>ainin<sub>g</sub> (Abdelnabi et al. 2024; Davidson et al. 2024), <sub>p</sub>rice ne<sub>g</sub>otiation (Xia et al. 2024; Fu et al. 2023; Liu, Gu, and Son<sub>g</sub> 2026), resource tradin<sub>g</sub> (Bianchi et al. 2024; Qian et al. 2026), and the ne<sub>g</sub>otiation of smart or self-executin<sub>g</sub> contracts (Go<sub>p</sub>inathan et al. 2026; W<sub>y</sub>se et al. 2026). In each of our six Catering environments, we run three <sub>repea</sub>t<sub>s o</sub>f th<sub>e or</sub>d<sub>ere</sub>d $3 \times 3$ <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>-pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>se</sub>t<sub>up.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>y</sub>i<sub>e</sub>ld<sub>s</sub> 162 negotiations, of which 159 reach agreement (98.1%).

• Utilit<sub>y</sub>: Realized <sub>p</sub>a<sub>y</sub>of<sub>;</sub> Compliant Utility removes <sub>p</sub>ositi<sub>ve</sub> <sub>payo</sub>f f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t <sub>v</sub>i<sub>o</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>per</sub>i<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s.</sub>

• Re<sub>g</sub>ret: Pa<sub>y</sub>of shortfall relative to RCC <sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> on the same history; Compliant Regret compares compliant utilities.

• Com<sub>p</sub>liance: Turn-level contract com<sub>p</sub>liance rate. Conditional measures the rate before any counterparty defection; Exploited measures the rate after counterparty defection; Tit-for-Tat measures the rate at which compliance/defecti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>me</sub>t <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> <sub>response.</sub>

• Defection: Fraction of <sub>g</sub>ames with an<sub>y</sub> contract violation. Unilateral means defecting w/o experiencing defection; Reciprocal means defecting after the counterparty defects fi<sub>rs</sub>t<sub>.</sub> C<sub>on</sub>diti<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>es are compu</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> f<sub>or games w</sub>ith t<sup>h</sup>e re<sup>l</sup>evant o<sub>pp</sub>ortun<sup>i</sup>t<sub>y</sub>.

## Catering negotiation system prompt—Caterer

<table><tr><td>GOAL</td></tr><tr><td>NEGOTIATION PROCESS: You have at most 50 rounds to reach an agreement.</td></tr><tr><td>Each round has two turns: the customer acts first, followed by the caterer.</td></tr><tr><td>On your turn, you may send a message to the other party and optionally propose a contract. You cannot formally accept a contract. If you agree with terms proposed by the customer, propose the complete agreed contract for the customer to formally accept on their next turn. The customer&#x27;s formal acceptance ends the negotiation with an agreement.</td></tr><tr><td>You may terminate the negotiation and walk away at any time. If either party terminates the negotiation, or if no agreement is reached within 50 rounds, negotiations end with no contract.</td></tr><tr><td>Negotiate a contract with the customer, explicitly negotiating: (i) price per unit of each dish, (ii) delivery schedule (how many of each dish per production round over 5 production rounds), and (iii) payment schedule (how much to pay in each of the 6 payment weeks).</td></tr><tr><td>Aim to negotiate a contract that maximizes your expected profit when executed during the subsequent contract-execution phase.</td></tr><tr><td>IMPORTANT: Any contract you propose MUST explicitly state the price per unit for each dish, the exact quantity of each dish per</td></tr><tr><td>production round, and the exact payment amount for each payment week — do not propose a contract that leaves any of these unspecified. All contracted dish quantities must be non-negative whole numbers, and all payment amounts must be non-negative whole-dollar</td></tr><tr><td>amounts (no fractions).</td></tr><tr><td>Only propose a contract once you believe you have gathered sufficient information from the negotiation to specify all of these terms.</td></tr><tr><td>PRIVATE INFO You are a caterer negotiating a catering contract with a prospective customer.</td></tr><tr><td>Your initial budget: $20.</td></tr><tr><td>RECIPES: You produce Margherita Pizza, Pesto Pasta, and Tomato Soup using Mozzarella, Basil, and Tomato.</td></tr><tr><td>Each Margherita Pizza needs 1 Tomato + 1 Mozzarella + 1 Basil, each Pesto Pasta needs 1 Mozzarella + 1 Basil, each Tomato Soup needs 1 Tomato.</td></tr><tr><td>All ingredient-purchase and dish-production quantities must be non-negative whole numbers.</td></tr><tr><td>ENVIRONMENT STOCHASTICITY: (a) Prices: each production round, each ingredient price is independently set to one of two values with equal probability (50/50) — Mozzarella $1 or $3/unit, Basil $1 or $2/unit, Tomato $2 or $3/unit. (b) Delivery loss: for each ingredient, if you order x units you receive between floor(x/2) and x units (uniformly at random). You pay for the full amount ordered, not the amount received. For example, ordering 6 Mozzarella means you pay for 6 but receive 3, 4, 5, or 6 with equal chance. (c) Spoilage:</td></tr><tr><td>inventory carried over from the previous production round independently loses 0, 1, or 2 units per ingredient per production round (equal probability, 1/3 each). Spoilage happens before you order new ingredients. (d) Inventory cap: you can store at most 10 units of each</td></tr><tr><td>ingredient. Any received units that would exceed this cap are lost — you still pay for them. This cap applies before production, so you cannot order beyond capacity to use the surplus in the same round. SCHEDULE: The <sub>g</sub>ame lasts 11 weeks. Odd weeks (1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 11) are PAYMENT weeks — the AI startu<sub>p</sub> founder <sub>p</sub>a<sub>y</sub>s <sub>y</sub>ou (6 total).   
Even weeks (2, 4, 6, 8, 10) are SERVICE weeks — <sub>y</sub>ou reserve resources and <sub>p</sub>rovide services (5 total).

SERVICE WEEK PROCESS (in order): (1) resource ex<sub>p</sub>iration occurs, (2) <sub>y</sub>ou reserve resources (ca<sub>p</sub>acit<sub>y</sub> shortfall a<sub>pp</sub>lies), (3) <sub>y</sub>ou <sub>p</sub>rovide services, (4) <sub>p</sub>rices are re-randomized for the next service round.

CONTRACT EXECUTION ACTIONS: Durin<sub>g</sub> contract execution, <sub>y</sub>our onl<sub>y</sub> actions are (i) reservin<sub>g</sub> resources and (ii) <sub>p</sub>rovidin<sub>g</sub> services. The AI startu<sub>p</sub> founder’s onl action is makin<sub>g p</sub>a ments. Pa ments must be non-ne<sub>g</sub>ative multi<sub>p</sub>les of \$1<sub>,</sub>000<sub>,</sub> includin<sub>g</sub> \$0. There is no refund mechanism. Unused resources carr<sub>y</sub> over between service rounds (subject to resource ex<sub>p</sub>iration). \pi _ { \mathrm { S u p p } , t } ^ { \mathrm { R C } } ( s , \mathbf { p } ) \in \left\{ \begin{array} { l l } { \displaystyle \operatorname* { m a x } _ { \mathbf { o } \in \mathcal { A } _ { t } ^ { \mathrm { s a t } } ( s , \mathbf { p } ) } \widehat { V } _ { t } ( s , \mathbf { p } , \mathbf { o } ) , } & { \mathcal { A } _ { t } ^ { \mathrm { s a t } } ( s , \mathbf { p } ) \neq \emptyset , } \\ { \displaystyle \operatorname* { m a x } _ { \mathbf { o } \in \mathcal { A } _ { t } ^ { \mathrm { o r d } } ( s , \mathbf { p } ) } \widehat { P } _ { t } ( s , \mathbf { p } , \mathbf { o } ) , } & { \mathcal { A } _ { t } ^ { \mathrm { s a t } } ( s , \mathbf { p } ) = \emptyset . } \end{array} \right.\tag{12}

{   
"contracts": [   
{

```jsonl
"contract_id": "<same id from input>",
"dish_prices": {"A": <number|null>, "B": <number|null>, "C": <number|null>},
"production_schedule": [
{"week": 2, "A": <int|null>, "B": <int|null>, "C": <int|null>},
{"week": 4, "A": <int|null>, "B": <int|null>, "C": <int|null>},
{"week": 6, "A": <int|null>, "B": <int|null>, "C": <int|null>},
{"week": 8, "A": <int|null>, "B": <int|null>, "C": <int|null>},
{"week": 10, "A": <int|null>, "B": <int|null>, "C": <int|null>}
],
"payment_schedule": [
{"week": 1, "amount": <number|null>},
{"week": 3, "amount": <number|null>},
{"week": 5, "amount": <number|null>},
{"week": 7, "amount": <number|null>},
{"week": 9, "amount": <number|null>},
{"week": 11, "amount": <number|null>}
],
"contingency_set": ["<implemented_clause_name>"],
"contingency_params": {

}

Mapping:
- A = Margherita Pizza
- B = Pesto Pasta
- C = Tomato Soup
```

## Rules:

\- Parse each input contract\_id exactly once and copy its identifier exactly. - ‘production\_schedule‘ must contain exactly one row for each of weeks 2, 4, 6, 8, and 10. Each row has ‘week‘, ‘A‘, ‘B‘, and ‘C‘; quantities are

‘payment\_schedule‘ must contain exactly one row for each of weeks 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, and 11. Each row has ‘week‘ and ‘amount‘; amounts are non-negative numbers or null.

- If the same quantities apply in weeks 2, 4, 6, 8, and 10, expand them.   
- If a fixed payment applies to multiple payment weeks, expand it.

\- Be especially careful that Week 11 is payment week 11, not week 1.

\- Do not invent missing values. Use null when absent or ambiguous.

\- Amounts and prices must be plain numbers, with no dollar signs.

\- Only return the fields shown above.

\- Extract only contingencies explicitly expressed by the contract. Do not add a default or implicit fallback contingency.

\- Include every implemented contingency explicitly present in the contract. Every array entry must be exactly one of: "substitution", "grim\_trigger", "payment\_deduction", or "rollover". Do not return any other clause name.

\- If the contract contains no explicit contingency, return ‘contingency\_set: []‘ and ‘contingency\_params: {}‘.

\- Use "grim\_trigger" only when the contract explicitly states termination, suspension of future performance, or an equivalent trigger after breach.

\- Use "substitution" when the contract explicitly allows some promised dishes above the required minimum quantities to be replaced with other dishes. Use expressly stated minimum quantities; if none are stated, use {"A": 1, "B": 1, "C": 1}.

\- Use "payment\_deduction" when a delivery shortfall results in a deduction from a future scheduled payment.

\- Use "rollover" when missed dishes carry forward or must be cured later. If the contract states a maximum deficit, extract that number; otherwise use null.

\- Only include parameter blocks for active contingencies. The permitted blocks

are ‘substitution: {"min\_qty": {"A": int, "B": int, "C": int}}‘,   
‘payment\_deduction: {"min\_qty": {"A": int, "B": int, "C": int}}‘, and   
‘rollover: {"max\_deficit": int|null}‘.

$$
\operatorname* { m a x } _ { \omega \in \Omega _ { \mathrm { b a s e } } ^ { \prime } } \ U _ { \mathrm { C u s t } } ( \omega ) + U _ { \mathrm { S u p p } } ( \omega )\tag{18}
$$

$$
\mathrm { s u b j e c t t o } \quad U _ { i } ( \omega ) \geq u _ { i } ( \perp ) ,
$$

$$
i \in \{ \mathrm { C u s t , S u p p } \} ,\tag{19}
$$

$$
\left| P _ { \mathrm { s a t } } ( \pi ^ { \mathrm { R C C } } , C ^ { \omega } ) - \rho \right| \leq 0 . 0 5 .\tag{20}

Thus the objective maximizes equal-weight expected mutual benefit, subject to individual rationality and membership in the target $P _ { \mathrm { s a t } }$ b<sub>an</sub>d<sub>.</sub> A <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>-s</sub>t<sub>ar</sub>t <sub>coor</sub>di<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e-ascen</sub>t <sub>searc</sub>h <sub>var</sub>i<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ree</sub> d<sub>e</sub>li<sub>very quan</sub>titi<sub>es,</sub> th<sub>ree un</sub>it <sub>pr</sub>i<sub>ces, an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>i<sub>x nom</sub>i<sub>na</sub>l <sub>paymen</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> We run this construction inde<sub>p</sub>endentl<sub>y</sub> in the four stochastic environments (Environments 3–6). $$

For model ℓ in role $i ,$ l<sub>e</sub>t $\mathcal { H } _ { \ell , i }$ b<sub>e</sub> it<sub>s eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>nego</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>or</sub>i<sub>es an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t $\mathcal { T } _ { i } ( h ) = \{ k : \mathrm { r o l e } _ { h } ( k ) = i \}$ i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>ex</sub> th<sub>e proposa</sub>l<sub>s</sub> it makes in history h. We average the feasibility of contract proposals across those histories and report:

$$

In an accepted negotiation, role i incurs acceptance regret if it passes over an earlier feasible counterparty <sub>o</sub>f<sub>er</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>g</sub>i<sub>ves</sub> it <sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>tl<sub>y grea</sub>t<sub>er expec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> th<sub>an</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>agreemen</sub>t<sub>,</sub> b<sub>eyon</sub>d th<sub>e numer</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l t<sub>o</sub>l<sub>erance:</sub>

$$

In production round t, the current price scenario is drawn at its true probability and the policy chooses $\mathbf { o } _ { t }$

Th<sub>e sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>paymen</sub>t i<sub>s a</sub>dd<sub>e</sub>d <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e rea</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>or</sub>d<sub>er cos</sub>t i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>bit<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub> P<sub>ro</sub>b<sub>a</sub>bilit<sub>y mass</sub> f<sub>or w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> S<sub>upp</sub>li<sub>er</sub> b<sub>a</sub>l<sub>ance</sub> is negative is routed to DEAD. We then spread probability mass over realized input receipts, apply the contract’s production and contingency rules, and route any uncured production shortfall to DEAD.

After th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>on roun</sub>d<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>paymen</sub>t <sub>amoun</sub>t <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>es on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>on cap an</sub>d i<sub>s no</sub>t <sub>o</sub>th<sub>erw</sub>i<sub>se cre</sub>dit<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e carr</sub>i<sub>e</sub>d balance. Survivin<sub>g</sub> inventor<sub>y</sub> is finall<sub>y</sub> s<sub>p</sub>read throu<sub>g</sub>h the true s<sub>p</sub>oila<sub>g</sub>e distribution to form the ste<sub>p</sub>−t occu<sub>p</sub>anc<sub>y</sub> measure $\rho _ { t }$

M<sub>e</sub>tri<sub>c.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $\begin{array} { r } { S _ { t } = \sum _ { s \mathrm { ~ l i v e } } \rho _ { t } ( s ) = 1 - \rho _ { t } ( \mathsf { D E A D } ) } \end{array}$ be the probabilit<sub>y</sub> mass surviving production round t (i.e. the total probabilit<sub>y</sub> that no contract violation will occur b<sub>y</sub> step t under the RCC polic<sub>y</sub>). We define

$$
\mathrm { C o m p l e t e n e s s } ( \omega ) = 1 - \frac { 1 } { | W _ { \mathrm { p r o d } } | } \sum _ { t = 1 } ^ { | W _ { \mathrm { p r o d } } | } ( 1 - S _ { t } ) \in [ 0 , 1 ] .\tag{30}
$$

This is one minus the expected fraction of the episode spent in breach of the contract. Since DEAD is absorbing, an earlier breach is charged in every subsequent round, while survival through all production rounds results in a score of 1.

d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng wee</sub>k<sub>s a</sub>ft<sub>er exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> thi<sub>s se</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

Utility. The realized utility of a trajectory $\tau _ { j }$ is just $u _ { i } ( \tau _ { j } ; \theta _ { i } )$ <sub>.</sub> F<sub>or</sub> <sub>comp</sub>li<sub>an</sub>t<sub>-u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> <sub>accoun</sub>ti<sub>ng,</sub> <sub>our</sub> i<sub>mp</sub>l<sub>emen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>ecomposes</sub> <sub>rea</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o per-wee</sub>k <sub>ga</sub>i<sub>ns:</sub>

$$
\Delta u _ { \mathrm { C u s t } , j , w } = \sum _ { d \in D } v _ { d } q _ { j , w + 1 , d } ^ { \prime } - M _ { j , w } ^ { \mathrm { p a i d } } ,
$$

$$
w \in W _ { \mathrm { p a y } } ,\tag{31}
$$

$$
\Delta u _ { \mathrm { S u p p } , j , w } = M _ { j , w - 1 } ^ { \mathrm { p a i d } } + \mathbf { 1 } [ w = L - 1 ] M _ { j , L } ^ { \mathrm { p a i d } } - \sum _ { x \in \mathcal { X } } o _ { j , w , x } p _ { j , w , x } ,
$$

$$
w \in W _ { \mathrm { p r o d } } ,\tag{32}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $q _ { j , 1 2 , d } ^ { \prime } = 0$ <sub>.</sub> Si<sub>nce</sub> th<sub>ere</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>more</sub> <sub>paymen</sub>t <sub>wee</sub>k th<sub>an</sub> <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>wee</sub>k<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> S<sub>upp</sub>li<sub>er</sub>’<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>wee</sub>k <sub>groups</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>paymen</sub>t<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>wee</sub>k <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e pr</sub>i<sub>or wee</sub>k<sub>.</sub> R<sub>ea</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>u</sub>tilit<sub>y can</sub> th<sub>en</sub> b<sub>e wr</sub>itt<sub>en as:</sub>

$$
u _ { i } ( \tau _ { j } ; \theta _ { i } ) = u _ { i } ( \bot ) + \sum _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i } } \Delta u _ { i , j , w } .\tag{33}
$$

Compliant utility excludes the positive gains that can be attributed to weeks where a contract constraint is violated:

$$
u _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { c } } ( \tau _ { j } ) = u _ { i } ( \perp ) + \sum _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i } } \left[ c _ { i , j , w } \Delta u _ { i , j , w } + ( 1 - c _ { i , j , w } ) \operatorname* { m i n } ( \Delta u _ { i , j , w } , 0 ) \right] .\tag{34}
$$

R<sub>eg</sub>r<sub>e</sub>t<sub>.</sub> R<sub>egre</sub>t <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs measure u</sub>tilit<sub>y o</sub>f <sub>some agen</sub>t <sub>re</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> t<sub>o w</sub>h<sub>a</sub>t i<sub>s o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> RCC b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $\tau _ { j } ^ { \mathrm { R C C } }$ b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> counterfactual trajectory obtained by replacing the agent’s policy in role i with RCC while holding fixed the contract, environment <sub>con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub>ti<sub>on, coun</sub>t<sub>erpar</sub>t<sub>y po</sub>li<sub>cy, an</sub>d RNG <sub>see</sub>d<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s a separa</sub>t<sub>e s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub> b<sub>ecause a c</sub>h<sub>ange</sub>d <sub>po</sub>li<sub>cy can c</sub>h<sub>ange</sub> RNG consum<sub>p</sub>t<sup>i</sup>on, $\bar { \tau } _ { j } ^ { \mathrm { R C C } }$ <sub>nee</sub>d <sub>no</sub>t <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>rea</sub>li<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>oc</sub>h<sub>as</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> hi<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ory</sub> <sub>o</sub>f $\tau _ { j }$ . Ex-post regret and compliant regret are, respectively,

$$
\mathrm { R e g r e t } _ { i } ( \tau _ { j } ) = u _ { i } ( \tau _ { j } ^ { \mathrm { R C C } } ; \theta _ { i } ) - u _ { i } ( \tau _ { j } ; \theta _ { i } ) ,\tag{35}
$$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathrm { R e g r e t } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { c } } ( \tau _ { j } ) = u _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { c } } ( \tau _ { j } ^ { \mathrm { R C C } } ) - u _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { c } } ( \tau _ { j } ) . } \end{array}\tag{36}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>comp</sub>li<sub>an</sub>t <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t <sub>app</sub>li<sub>es</sub> <sub>comp</sub>li<sub>an</sub>t <sub>u</sub>tilit<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e</sub> RCC <sub>re</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>agen</sub>t<sub>.</sub>

D<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> D<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>agen</sub>t’<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>v</sub>i<sub>o</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on wee</sub>k b<sub>y</sub>

$$
f _ { i , j } = \operatorname* { m i n } \{ w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } : c _ { i , j , w } = 0 \} ,\tag{37}
$$

<sub>w</sub>ith $f _ { i , j } = \infty$ if i never violates. Recalling the violation indicator $D _ { i , t }$ <sub>,</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>run-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>exe</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>pre-ac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>erpar</sub>t <sub>as:</sub>

$$
D _ { i , j , w } = { \bf 1 } [ f _ { - i , j } < w ] ,\tag{38}
$$

which records whether role i has observed a counterparty violation in a strictly earlier week. The focal agent’s pre- and <sub>p</sub>ost-v<sup>i</sup>o<sup>l</sup>at<sup>i</sup>on o<sub>pp</sub>ortun<sup>i</sup>t<sub>y</sub> sets are:

$$
\mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } = \{ w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } : D _ { i , j , w } = 0 \} ,\tag{39}
$$

$$
\mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } = \{ w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } : D _ { i , j , w } = 1 \} .\tag{40}
$$

Th<sub>us a same-wee</sub>k <sub>v</sub>i<sub>o</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> i<sub>s no</sub>t <sub>a response</sub> t<sub>o an ear</sub>li<sub>er v</sub>i<sub>o</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t<sub>:</sub>

$$
e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } = \mathbf { 1 } [ \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } \neq \emptyset ] ,\tag{41}
$$

$$
e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e s p } } = \mathbf { 1 } [ f _ { - i , j } < f _ { i , j } \land \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } \neq \emptyset ] .\tag{42}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>secon</sub>d i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>or</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>res</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>erpar</sub>t<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o</sub> b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>t fi<sub>rs</sub>t d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>agen</sub>t t<sub>o</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> <sub>a</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>er</sub> <sub>oppor</sub>t<sub>un</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> D<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e run-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>ors:</sub>

$$
z _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { a n y } } = \mathbf { 1 } [ f _ { i , j } < \infty ] ,\tag{43}
$$

$$
z _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { u n i } } = \mathbf { 1 } [ \exists w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } : c _ { i , j , w } = 0 ] ,\tag{44}
$$

$$
z _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e c } } = \mathbf { 1 } [ e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e s p } } = 1 \ \wedge \ \exists w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } : c _ { i , j , w } = 0 ] .\tag{45}
$$

O<sub>ur</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs are</sub> th<sub>en</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>as:</sub>

$$
\mathrm { D e f e c t i o n } _ { i } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } } z _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { a n y } } } { \vert \mathcal { R } \vert } ,\tag{46}
$$

$$
{ \mathrm { U n i l a t e r a l } } _ { i } = { \frac { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } } z _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { u n i } } } { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } } } ,\tag{47}
$$

$$
{ \mathrm { R e c i p r o c a l } } _ { i } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } } z _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e c } } } { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e s p } } } .\tag{48}
$$

In words, the raw defection metric Defection measures the rate at which the agent defects across all runs, the unilateral defection metric Unilateral measures the defection rate across runs where the counterparty did not defect first, and the reciprocal defection metric Reciprocal measures the defection rate across runs where the counterparty defected first.

C<sub>o</sub>m<sub>p</sub>li<sub>a</sub>n<sub>ce:</sub> M<sub>ac</sub>r<sub>o-ave</sub>r<sub>ag</sub>in<sub>g.</sub> All <sub>aggrega</sub>t<sub>e me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>use macro-averag</sub>i<sub>ng across per</sub>f<sub>ormance runs.</sub> F<sub>or a g</sub>i<sub>ven</sub> f<sub>oca</sub>l<sub>-agen</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>coun</sub>t<sub>erpar</sub>t<sub>y pa</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ng, eac</sub>h $j \in \mathcal R$ i<sub>s one o</sub>f th<sub>e env</sub>i<sub>ronmen</sub>t<sub>-con</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t <sub>runs.</sub> Utilit<sub>y an</sub>d <sub>regre</sub>t <sub>a</sub>l<sub>rea</sub>d<sub>y</sub> i<sub>e</sub>ld <sub>one sca</sub>l<sub>ar er case an</sub>d <sub>are avera e</sub>d di<sub>rec</sub>tl <sub>across cases;</sub> th<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ree</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on es</sub>ti<sub>man</sub>d<sub>s a</sub>b<sub>ove ana</sub>l<sub>o ous</sub>l <sub>avera e one</sub> bi<sub>nary</sub> i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>or per e</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ibl<sub>e run.</sub>

F<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>our</sub> <sub>wee</sub>k<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>comp</sub>li<sub>ance</sub> <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>cs,</sub> <sub>we</sub> fi<sub>rs</sub>t <sub>ca</sub>l<sub>cu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>w</sub>ithi<sub>n</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>run</sub> <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>en</sub> <sub>average</sub> th<sub>ose</sub> <sub>ra</sub>t<sub>es</sub> <sub>across</sub> <sub>e</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>runs.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $\mathcal { R } _ { i } = \{ j \in \mathcal { R } : \vert \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } \vert > 0 \}$ <sub>an</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne:</sub>

$$
\bar { c } _ { i , j } = \frac { \sum _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } } c _ { i , j , w } } { | \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } | } ,\tag{49}
$$

$$
\bar { c } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } = \frac { \sum _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } } c _ { i , j , w } } { | \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } | } ,\tag{50}
$$

$$
\bar { c } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } = \frac { \sum _ { w \in \mathscr { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } } c _ { i , j , w } } { | \mathscr { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } | } ,\tag{51}
$$

$$
\bar { m } _ { i , j } = \frac { 1 } { \vert \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } \vert } \sum _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } } \mathbf { 1 } [ 1 - c _ { i , j , w } = D _ { i , j , w } ] ,\tag{52}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>pre-</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>-ra</sub>t<sub>e</sub> i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> it<sub>s</sub> d<sub>enom</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>or</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>nonzero.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> <sub>macro-average</sub>d <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>man</sub>d<sub>s</sub> <sub>are:</sub>

$$
\mathrm { C o m p l i a n c e } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m a c r o } } = \frac { 1 } { \vert \mathcal { R } _ { i } \vert } \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } _ { i } } \bar { c } _ { i , j } ,\tag{53}
$$

$$
\mathrm { C o n d i t i o n a l } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m a c r o } } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } \bar { c } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } } { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } } ,\tag{54}
$$

$$
{ \mathrm { E x p l o i t e d } } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m a c r o } } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e s p } } \bar { c } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } } { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e s p } } } ,\tag{55}
$$

$$
\mathrm { T F T } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m a c r o } } = \frac { 1 } { \vert \mathcal { R } _ { i } \vert } \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } _ { i } } \bar { m } _ { i , j } .\tag{56}
$$

In words, the raw compliance metric Compliance measures the fraction of weeks in a run where the agent complies with the contract, the conditional compliance metric Conditional measures the fraction of weeks where the agent complies out of weeks where the counterparty is compliant, the exploited compliance metric Exploited measures the fraction of weeks where the agent complies out of weeks where the counterparty has defected, and the tit-for-tat metric TFT measures the fraction of weeks where th<sub>e agen</sub>t <sub>comp</sub>li<sub>es or</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n response</sub> t<sub>o comp</sub>li<sub>ance or</sub> d<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ec</sub>ti<sub>on respec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y.</sub>

Si<sub>nce we average across runs, e</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ibl<sub>e run rece</sub>i<sub>ves equa</sub>l <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>even w</sub>h<sub>en run con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>num</sub>b<sub>ers o</sub>f <sub>e</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ibl<sub>e wee</sub>k<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>ese are</sub> th<sub>e es</sub>ti<sub>man</sub>d<sub>s repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> t<sub>ex</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> D<sub>.</sub>3<sub>;</sub> th<sub>e superscr</sub>i<sub>p</sub>t “<sub>macro</sub>” i<sub>s om</sub>itt<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>di<sub>ngs.</sub>

C<sub>omp</sub>li<sub>ance:</sub> P<sub>oo</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>-wee</sub>k <sub>m</sub>i<sub>cro-averag</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> A<sub>s a sens</sub>iti<sub>v</sub>it <sub>ana</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>s, we a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>ll <sub>m</sub>i<sub>cro-average</sub> th<sub>e same wee</sub>k<sub>-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>ors,</sub> <sub>g</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>every</sub> <sub>e</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>wee</sub>k <sub>equa</sub>l <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>g</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>every</sub> <sub>e</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ibl<sub>e</sub> <sub>per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> <sub>run</sub> <sub>equa</sub>l <sub>we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht<sub>:</sub>

$$
\mathrm { C o m p l i a n c e } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m i c r o } } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } _ { i } } \sum _ { w \in { \mathcal { W } } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } } c _ { i , j , w } } { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } _ { i } } | { \mathcal { W } } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } | } ,\tag{57}
$$

$$
\mathrm { C o n d i t i o n a l } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m i c r o } } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } \sum _ { w \in \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } } c _ { i , j , w } } { \sum _ { j \in \mathcal { R } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } \vert \mathcal { W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p r e } } \vert } ,\tag{58}
$$

$$
\mathrm { E x p l o i t e d } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m i c r o } } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e s p } } \sum _ { w \in { \mathcal { W } } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } } c _ { i , j , w } } { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal { R } } } e _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { r e s p } } \vert { \mathcal { W } } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { p o s t } } \vert } ,\tag{59}
$$

$$
\mathrm { T F T } _ { i } ^ { \mathrm { m i c r o } } = \frac { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal R } _ { i } } \sum _ { w \in { \mathcal W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } } \mathbf { 1 } [ 1 - c _ { i , j , w } = D _ { i , j , w } ] } { \sum _ { j \in { \mathcal R } _ { i } } | { \mathcal W } _ { i , j } ^ { \mathrm { e v a l } } | } .\tag{60}
$$
